h7RgaNUCbF	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	我{{c1::是::link verb: to be (noun = noun)}}学生。	I'm a student.	Form	学生 is a noun, so 是 is needed to link it to 我. The copula 是 equates two noun phrases and never changes for person or time (我是, 他们是, 昨天是); its only negative is 不是.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-001	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi
Jdlfe;6jgj	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	他{{c1::不是::negative: is not}}老师。	He isn't a teacher.	Form	老师 is a noun after the copula, so the negative is 不是. 是 always takes 不, even about the past: 他以前不是老师 (he didn't use to be a teacher). 没是 doesn't exist.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-002	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi
"ey.m#Z>pKP"	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	那{{c1::是::link verb: to be}}我们的学校。	That's our school.	Form	The bare demonstrative 那 is the subject, and 是 links it to the noun phrase 我们的学校. Used as a pronoun 那 needs no measure word; before a noun it does: 那个学校. Institutions often drop 的 after a pronoun: 我们学校.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-003	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi
uc|$,91%vi	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	我{{c1::是::是 or 很?}}中国人。<br>我{{c1::很::是 or 很?}}忙。	I'm Chinese. / I'm busy.	Choice	中国人 is a noun, so it takes 是; 忙 is an adjective, which is a predicate in its own right and takes no copula. The 很 before an adjective is largely structural here rather than meaning 'very'.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-004	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi-hen	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi-hen
h2`bsmDrA/	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	今天{{c1::很::是 or 很?}}冷。	It's cold today.	Choice	冷 is an adjective, so no 是. A bare 今天冷 implies a contrast (today is cold, unlike yesterday); 很 makes it a plain statement, and only when stressed does it mean 'very'.	我是忙 is a classic learner error: adjectives never take 是 as a plain description.	Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-005	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi-hen	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::shi-hen
Gs3W;VBd&x	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	我{{c1::有::have}}一个哥哥。	I have an older brother.	Form	With a person as subject, 有 expresses possession, and the object takes number + measure word (一个哥哥). Siblings are always split by relative age: 哥哥 / 弟弟, 姐姐 / 妹妹.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-006	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::you	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::you
Dha.x/|04m	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	我{{c1::没有::don't have}}钱。	I don't have any money.	Form	The English 'don't' is a trap: 有 is negated only with 没, whatever the time reference, so 不有 is never possible. In speech 有 can drop after 没: 我没钱 is just as natural. The noun after existential 有 is new information and usually carries number + measure word: 两张床, with 张 for flat things such as beds and tables. N-.EybbLOJ	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	你{{c1::有::have}}时间吗？	Do you have time?	Form	Abstract possessions such as 时间 also take 有. The A-not-A version of this question is 你有没有时间?, because the negative of 有 is 没有; you can't mix the two forms with 吗.		Neutral		ZH-HSK1-01-009	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::you	Chinese::HSK1::grammar::you

Form	A known person's location is expressed with 在 + place as the main verb; no other verb is needed. Kinship terms after a pronoun normally drop 的 (我妈妈), and 家 takes no location word after 在. Form	As a question word, 哪儿 sits in the answer's slot, right after 在. 哪儿 is typical of northern speech; southern and Taiwanese speakers favour 哪里, which is also the usual written form. Form	医院 is a specific, known building, so 在 locates it. 学校 alone can follow 在 (在学校), but a relative position needs a location word: 学校前面, or 前边 in speech. K6Xa7G$%BL	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::01 是, 有 and 在	我的书{{c1::在::在 or 有?}}桌子上。<br>桌子上{{c1::有::在 or 有?}}一本书。	My book is on the table. / There's a book on the table.	Choice	Word order decides: a known thing first (我的书) takes 在 + place; a place first takes 有 + new thing. So the same book and table produce two different verbs depending on what's already known. Choice	Place first (学校里), so 有. 很多 goes straight before the noun without a measure word (很多学生), though 很多个 is heard in casual speech. With 有, 学校 and 学校里 are both acceptable subjects. Form	'Several of us' gives the first-person plural: 我 + suffix 们. 们 attaches only to pronouns and nouns for people (老师们), and it's dropped whenever a number is present. QFdO%Tc(/H	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::02 Pronouns, 这 / 那, 的, 叫 / 姓	(Polite, to a teacher) {{c1::您::you (polite)}}好！	Hello!	Form	Addressing a teacher calls for 您, the honorific second person used for elders, teachers, customers and strangers. t*l^JKT?Ra	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Chinese::HSK1::02 Pronouns, 这 / 那, 的, 叫 / 姓	(About a woman) {{c1::她::she}}是我的老师。	She's my teacher.	Form	The cue tells you the referent is female, which matters only in writing: 他, 她 and 它 are all pronounced tā. The character 她 was popularised around 1920 (by the linguist Liu Bannong); speech doesn't mark gender at all.
